▶ position manager: structural and broker protection

  ✔ thesis deterioration (3 adverse closes, beyond half R and the entry bar) exits early with a smaller loss than invalidation (0.3732ms)

✔ position manager: structural and broker protection (2.4153ms)
▶ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT
  ✔ profit milestone detection: PROFIT_AVAILABLE at the first close with MFE >= trigger R (0.75 R for D1, 1.0 R for D2) (0.4944ms)
  ✔ HOLD: with no profit available and no deterioration the manager holds and leaves protection unchanged (0.1585ms)
  ✔ HARVEST on weak continuation: a spike to the trigger followed by a weak close banks at that close (0.2472ms)
  ✔ PROTECT + RUN on strong continuation: floor activates from the next bar, ratchets upward, and the floor touch banks the floor (0.2331ms)
  ✔ EXIT on adverse break during a protected run (harvest at the close, never below the floor) (0.2128ms)
  ✔ HARVEST when the remaining reward compresses (distance to TP2 < 0.5 ATR) (0.1087ms)
  ✔ simple bank (CH-B) closes at the first close after 1 R (0.0801ms)

✔ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT (2.0304ms)

  ✔ releaseCdpLock never removes another process's lock, even a stale one it did not itself reclaim (3.7783ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock never throws even if the lock file is already gone (1.336ms)
✔ cdpLock: tryAcquireCdpLock() basic acquire/release (21.2329ms)
▶ cdpLock: stale-lock reclaim

  ✔ reclaims a lock older than maxAgeMs even if the holder PID still reports alive ("no permanent stale lock") (3.6155ms)
  ✔ does NOT reclaim a fresh lock from a still-alive holder (2.7984ms)

  ✔ tryAcquireCdpLock throws CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT if the CURRENT process already holds the lock (2.9334ms)
  ✔ withCdpLock rejects with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT (not a hang) if fn() tries to re-acquire the SAME lock nested inside itself (4.7693ms)
✔ cdpLock: re-entrancy -- fails fast, never deadlocks (7.9271ms)
▶ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout
  ✔ polls until a busy lock frees up, then acquires it (proves serialization: a second "operation" WAITS its turn rather than racing) (50.7741ms)
  ✔ throws CDP_LOCK_TIMEOUT rather than hanging forever if the lock never frees up (42.3998ms)
✔ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout (93.7148ms)
▶ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases
  ✔ releases after fn() resolves successfully (6.1693ms)
  ✔ releases after fn() throws (a WAIT-shaped/error result never leaves the lock held) (7.8848ms)
  ✔ two sequential withCdpLock() calls on the SAME path never see each other's lock (serialized, not deadlocked) (11.458ms)
  ✔ proves cross-process mutual exclusion via a simulated different-pid holder: a competing acquisition never wins while the (other-process) holder is live and fresh, and wins immediately once it is not (15.0079ms)
  ✔ within the SAME process, a second (non-nested but concurrent) withCdpLock() call on the SAME path fails fast with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT rather than hanging or double-entering fn() (35.0934ms)
  ✔ leaves no leftover .tmp- files behind after normal sequential use (Windows-race hardening cleans up its own losing temp files) (47.0555ms)
✔ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases (123.4332ms)

▶ setup / trigger rules on synthetic bars (information-time)

▶ P4B runtime-safety: every array.size()-bounded for-loop is guarded against an empty array
  ✔ every "for i = 0 to array.size(IDENT) - 1" is immediately preceded (skipping blank/comment lines) by "if array.size(IDENT) > 0" (2.3674ms)
  ✔ the P5 OUTCOME RESOLUTION backward loop (for safe in-loop array.remove) is guarded against an empty openSignals array exactly like every forward loop (0.6951ms)
  ✔ array.remove() now legitimately exists (P5 review/fix), but ONLY as the one safe in-loop removal inside the guarded, backward-iterating P5 resolution loop — no other unsafe indexed-collection function (array.first/array.last/array.pop/array.insert) exists anywhere (1.3803ms)
  ✔ array.shift() call sites remain safe: P2's two capacity-eviction sites remain guarded by "size > maxActiveLevels" preceded by array.push() (guaranteeing size >= 1); P5's recentHistory (closed-only) eviction is guarded by "size >= p5MaxTrackedSignals" instead (a capacity check ahead of the push, not a post-push overflow check — still provably safe, since array.shift() on an empty array is never reachable there either: the guard only ever removes an EXISTING oldest element to make room, and array.push() unconditionally follows it in the same block). Critically, openSignals (the never-evicted OPEN tracker) must have NO array.shift() call anywhere. (2.3292ms)
  ✔ the debug-table convenience reads (last-level consumed/swept) remain guarded by their own ternary size>0 check — unchanged by this patch (0.748ms)
  ✔ f_nearestAbove and f_nearestBelow (SL/TP2 structural-anchor helpers) are also guarded (0.5992ms)
✔ P4B runtime-safety: every array.size()-bounded for-loop is guarded against an empty array (9.5198ms)
